- `good-cause-notice`: `edu-no-good-cause-notice-in`; Absent; Absent (G4006): no good-cause eviction law or notice; local good-cause rules preempted (Ind. Code § 32-31-1-20(c)(4), (6)) except reduced-rent subsidized housing; federal voucher grounds and notice (24 C.F.R. § 982.310).
- `governmental-fines`: `tenant-caused-fines-as` (tag), `edu-governmental-fines-in`; Tagged; Tagged tenant-caused-fines-as (optional) plus education: no bar on passing tenant-caused fines (G3022); emergency-call penalties barred or tenant-assessed and capped at $250; notice and cure before local penalties; cleanup liens on owner.
- `guarantor-renewal`: `guaranty-as` (tag), `edu-guarantor-renewal-in`; Tagged; Tagged guaranty-as (optional) plus education: no guarantor-renewal statute (G3036); Ind. Code ch. 34-22-1 lets a surety force prompt suit by written notice or be discharged; signed writing per Ind. Code § 32-21-1-1.
- `guest-rights`: `edu-no-guest-rights-statute-in`; Absent; Absent (G7026): no guest-rights statute; lease decides; emergency possessory order for a guest's crime (seven days); retaliation exception for guest-caused violations; invitees are not squatters.
- `habitability-materiality`: `edu-habitability-materiality-in`; Present; Present: no materiality threshold on the repair action (Ind. Code § 32-31-8-6; G1005); seriousness tests only for emergency orders and retaliation (Ind. Code § 32-31-6-6, Ind. Code § 32-31-8.5-2, Ind. Code § 32-31-8.5-5).
- `habitability-modifiable`: `edu-habitability-modifiable-in`; Present; Present: no landlord duty is modifiable (Ind. Code § 32-31-8-4); item duties reach only what is provided at signing; utilities payer set by lease (Ind. Code § 32-31-5-6(c)); no modification statute (G1004).
- `habitability-presumption`: `edu-no-habitability-presumption-in`; Absent; Absent: no habitability, repair or retaliation presumption (G1006); only the occupancy-standard presumption (Ind. Code § 32-31-8-7) and the consumer act's signed-contract knowledge presumption.
- `habitability-statement`: `edu-no-habitability-statement-in`; Absent; Absent: no required habitability statement (G1007); duty applies anyway and includes what the lease promises (Ind. Code § 32-31-8-5(1)); required lease statements are flood plain and military installation.
- `habitability-waiver`: `edu-habitability-waiver-in`; Present; Present: Ind. Code § 32-31-8-4 voids any waiver of the landlord-duty chapter; no rent-reduction or separate-document route (G1002); smoke detector rules separately non-waivable.
- `hazardous-contamination-disclosure`: `edu-environmental-covenant-notice-in`, `environmental-covenant-notice-in`; Present; Present, conditional: no general contamination disclosure (G6038), but post-2009 environmental restrictive covenants must require notice to transferees of an interest in the land (Ind. - `health-district-rental-rules`: `edu-no-health-district-rental-rules-in`; Absent; Absent as a copy or referral duty (G4013); health-and-housing-code duties (Ind. Code § 32-31-8-5) and health department unfit-dwelling orders served on tenant and owner (Ind. Code ch. 16-41-20) present.
- `heating`: `edu-heating-in`; Present; Present (conditional): heating system, if provided, must adequately supply heat at all times (Ind. Code § 32-31-8-5(4)(D)); no minimum temperature (G5023); boiler permits by statute, boiler rules not held (fetch filed, non-blocking).
- `hoa`: `edu-hoa-in`; Present; Present: condo tenants bound (Ind. Code § 32-25-1-2); homestead-only votes on HOA rental restrictions (Ind. Code § 32-25.5-3-11(d)); fine schedules (Ind. - `holding-deposit`: `edu-no-holding-deposit-rule-in`; Absent; Absent (G3012): no holding-deposit statute; pre-lease money may sit outside the deposit definition until a lease, case law unread; broker companies must trust-account funds of others (finding on edu-no-deposit-holding-rule-in).
- `home-business`: `edu-home-business-in`, `home-business-permission-co` (tag); Absent; Absent as a right or lease limit (G4034); home based vendor food sales exempt and free of local licensing (Ind. Code ch. 16-42-5.3); CO home-business permission clause tagged.
- `homestead-waiver`: `edu-homestead-waiver-in`; Present; Present: exemptions under Ind. Const. art. 1, § 22 and Ind. - `infirmity-termination`: `edu-no-infirmity-termination-in`; Absent; Absent (G2006): no Indiana rule ending a tenancy on incapacity or a move to care; Ind. Code § 32-31-1-23 representative duties, periodic notice, SCRA injury exception and fair-housing accommodation noted.
- `informal-dispute-resolution`: `edu-informal-dispute-resolution-in`, `informal-dispute-resolution-or` (tag); Present; Present (limits): no statute on informal-dispute lease terms (G8018); eviction diversion must be voluntary (Ind. - `inspection-condemnation-disclosure`: `edu-code-order-disclosure-in`; Present; Present, conditional: unmet unsafe-building order must be disclosed to a taker of a substantial property interest, with notice to the authority in 5 days (Ind. - `inspection-notice-penalty`: `edu-no-inspection-notice-penalty-in`; Absent; Absent as a fixed penalty (G7020; G7019 failed a positive): emergency possessory order, later damages hearing, other claims; complaint about entry is protected activity. - `jury-waiver`: `edu-jury-waiver-in`; Present; Present: jury right inviolate (Ind. Const. art. 1, § 20); waived by small claims filing or untimely demand; no statute on pre-dispute lease waivers (G1012); clause not offered (case law).
- `key-control-policy`: `edu-no-key-control-mandate-in`; Absent; Absent (G7021): no key-control policy or staff background-check mandate; entry rules bind anyone entering for the landlord; protected tenants' 24-hour lock change.
- `knowing-use-penalty`: `edu-knowing-use-penalty-in`; Present; Present (narrow): no landlord-tenant penalty for void terms (G1018); cognovit Class B misdemeanor (Ind. Code § 34-54-4-1); consumer act unconscionable and false-rights representations with Attorney General civil penalty.
- `landlord-breach-remedy`: `edu-landlord-breach-remedy-in`; Present; Present: court remedies only (Ind. Code § 32-31-8-6 repair suit, Ind. Code ch. 32-31-6 emergency orders, deposit and disclosure remedies); no withholding, escrow or repair-and-deduct (G1026).
- `landlord-liability-insurance`: `edu-no-landlord-insurance-mandate-in`; Absent; Absent (G4031): no landlord liability insurance mandate or disclosure; insurer inspections can qualify a managed unit for the local inspection exemption (Ind. Code § 36-1-20-4.1(c)).
- `landlord-registration`: `edu-landlord-registration-in`; Absent; Absent as a state rule (G4002); local registration limited by Ind. Code ch. 36-1-20 ($5 fee, free permit, pre-1984 programs exempt); unsafe-building owner contact duty (Ind. Code § 36-7-9-29).
- `landlord-remedies-termination`: `edu-landlord-remedies-termination-in`; Present; Present: possession by ejectment action (small claims if rent due at most $10,000), money for rent, use and occupation and damages, waste forfeiture; small claims waives the excess; no acceleration or future-rent statute (G2011).
- `landlord-self-cure`: `edu-no-landlord-self-cure-in`, `landlord-self-cure-in`; Absent; Absent: no statutory self-cure right (G1029); Ind. - `late-fee-limit`: `edu-no-late-fee-limit-in`; Absent; Absent (G3018): no residential or mobile home late-fee cap or grace period; self-storage ($20 or 20%) and rent-to-own goods caps only; mobile home community lien limited to 60 days' late rent against a lienholder.
- `law-enforcement-cooperation`: `edu-law-enforcement-cooperation-in`; Present; Present: no crime-free or landlord-police statute (G2023); universal child-abuse (Ind. - `lead-state-notices`: `edu-no-lead-state-notices-in`; Absent; Absent (G6018, Code plus 410 Ind. Admin. - `lease-completeness`: `edu-no-lease-completeness-rule-in`; Absent; Absent (G6005): no blank-space rule; consumer act covers leases but lists no blank item; rent-to-own blank rule excludes dwellings (Ind. Code § 24-7-1-7).
- `lease-content-requirements`: `edu-lease-content-requirements-in`; Present; Present, short: flood and military statements in the lease; agent names, water/sewer disclosure and smoke-detector acknowledgment in any writing; no first-page or term-date rule (G6004).
- `lease-notice-initial-requirement`: `edu-no-lease-initials-requirement-in`; Absent; Absent (G6006): no initials or separate-signature rule for lease terms; smoke-detector acknowledgment and water/sewer signed page are the only signed writings; signing raises a knowledge presumption (Ind. - `lease-registration`: `edu-lease-registration-in`; Present; Present: lease over three years recorded within 45 days or void against later good-faith buyer, lessee or mortgagee (Ind. Code § 32-31-2-1, Ind. Code § 32-31-2-2); memorandum allowed (Ind. Code § 36-2-11-20).
- `lease-term-limitation`: `edu-no-lease-term-limit-in`; Absent; Absent as a cap (G7009; G7008 failed a positive): no maximum term or seal rule; leases over three years need a signed writing and recording in 45 days; a married man may lease his own land without his wife; entireties case law, all owners sign.
- `lease-type-parity`: `edu-no-lease-type-parity-in`; Absent; Absent (G7011; G7010 failed a positive): no parity rule between month-to-month and fixed-term tenancies; general limits only (fair housing, retaliation exceptions, 30-day modification notice); local rent and lease-term rules preempted except subsidized housing.
- `lease-type-size`: `edu-no-lease-type-size-in`; Absent; Absent for the lease (G6043): only the water/sewer disclosure font rule (Ind. - `limitation-of-actions`: `edu-limitation-of-actions-in`; Present; Present: six years for rents and written money contracts (Ind. Code § 34-11-2-7, Ind. Code § 34-11-2-9), ten for other written contracts (Ind. Code § 34-11-2-11); signed written acknowledgment (Ind. Code § 34-11-9-1).
- `liquidated-damages`: `edu-no-liquidated-damages-rule-in`; Absent; Absent: no liquidated-damages statute for leases (G1017; penalty doctrine is case law); deposit covers rent due for premature termination; protected tenants owe no early-termination fees (Ind. Code § 32-31-9-12(d)).
- `lockout-for-rent-delinquency`: `edu-no-lockout-for-rent-in`; Present; Present (prohibition): no lockout or utility shutoff except by court order (Ind. Code § 32-31-5-6(c)); abandonment exception; tenant emergency possessory order within 3 business days.
- `maintenance-duty-shift`: `edu-maintenance-duty-shift-in`; Absent; Absent: no statute shifts landlord maintenance duties to the tenant (G1028); duties non-waivable (Ind. Code § 32-31-8-4); lease may rely on tenant duties (Ind. Code § 32-31-7-5) and assign routine upkeep outside them.
- `meter-conservation-charge`: `edu-no-meter-conservation-charge-in`; Absent; Absent (G5015): no meter-attached efficiency charge or successor notice; sub-billed water limited to usage plus listed fees (170 Ind. Admin. Code 15-2-0.5(b)); electric submeter charges capped (Ind. Code § 8-1-2-36.5(c)).
- `minor-tenant-filing`: `edu-no-minor-tenant-rule-in`; Absent; Absent (G2019): majority at 18 (Ind. Code § 1-1-4-5); no minor-lease or minor-filing statute; juvenile-court emancipation may empower contracting; Ind. Trial Rule 17(C) guardian ad litem.

- `nonrefundable-deposit-separate-notice`: `edu-no-nonrefundable-separate-notice-in`; Absent; Absent (G3014, G3013): no separate-notice rule for nonrefundable amounts; points to edu-nonrefundable-deposit-notice-in and the tagged nonrefundable-fees-wv.
- `nonresident-owner-agent`: `edu-nonresident-owner-agent-in`; Present; Present: Indiana-resident manager and agent disclosure (Ind. - `notice-service-fee`: `edu-no-notice-service-fee-rule-in`; Absent; Absent (G3025): no notice-service fee statute and no codified contract-penalty rule for real property (Ind. Code § 26-1-2-718 is goods only); no clause offered; points to edu-no-eviction-penalty-rule-in.
- `notice-to-quit-waiver`: `edu-notice-to-quit-waiver-in`; Present; Present: only the 10-day nonpayment notice admits agreement (Ind. Code § 32-31-1-6; clause not offered); periodic, year-to-year and at-will notices have no waiver rule either way (G1020); cautious course: do not shorten.
- `notice-to-vacate-additional-terms`: `edu-no-notice-to-vacate-terms-rule-in`; Absent; Absent (G7016): no rules on alterations or conduct while a notice to vacate is pending; access, services, entry, repair and retaliation rules continue until the tenancy ends.
- `optional-lease-terms`: `edu-optional-lease-terms-in`; Present; Present: lease-invoked options listed; lease with option to buy (up to 4 units) triggers seller disclosure form before acceptance (Ind. - `ordnance-demolition-meter-disclosures`: `edu-ordnance-demolition-meter-disclosures-in`; Present; Present: no ordnance or demolition disclosure (demolition is a retaliation exception, Ind. Code 15-2-0.5, 15-2-3).
- `other-landlord-facilities`: `edu-other-landlord-facilities-in`; Present; Present: elevators need annual state operating certificates, displayed, with licensed maintenance (Ind. Code § 22-15-5-4); garage door opener reversal test and tag for suppliers (Ind. Code § 24-5-18-8).
- `owner-move-in-reservation`: `edu-owner-move-in-in`, `owner-occupancy-termination-ar` (tag); Tagged; Tagged owner-occupancy-termination-ar plus IN education row: no just-cause or owner move-in rule; mid-term owner occupancy only if the lease reserves it; retaliation good-faith exception only at end of term; MH community closure 180 days.
- `parking-rules-notice`: `edu-no-parking-rules-notice-in`; Absent; Absent (G7029; G7028 too broad, superseded): no parking-rules notice, permit or registration-tow waiting rule; 30-day modification notice; abandoned-vehicle towing; commercial tow-away zone signs (P.L. 81-2026, July 1, 2026).
- `part5-nonwaivable`: `edu-part5-nonwaivable-in`; Present; Present: chapter-by-chapter non-waiver sentences in art. 32-31 plus smoke detector, abandonment, attornment, cognovit, small claims venue and arbitration-counsel rules; no sale-leaseback rule (G1009).
- `periodic-services-entry`: `edu-no-periodic-services-entry-rule-in`; Absent; Absent (G7018): no no-notice entry for scheduled services; every non-emergency entry needs reasonable notice at reasonable times (non-waivable); scheduled-services-entry-la not tagged (open fill-ins).
- `pest-control-notice`: `edu-no-pest-control-notice-in`; Absent; Absent (G5042): no pesticide notice for rentals; notification registry for schools only (Ind. Code § 15-16-5-27.5); 357 IAC 1 not held.
- `pet-eviction-fact-sheet`: `edu-no-pet-eviction-fact-sheet-in`; Absent; Absent (G6042): no pet-and-eviction fact sheet or pet-in-eviction rule; guide dog and assistance animal rows apply.
- `plain-language`: `edu-no-plain-language-rule-in`; Absent; Absent for leases (G6008): no readability, caption or front-page rule; consumer act unconscionability reaches leases without a dollar limit; narrow clear-language rule for housing with services contracts (Ind. Code § 12-10-15-9).

- `pool-safety`: `edu-pool-safety-in`; Present; Present: apartment, condominium and mobile home park pools are semi-public pools (410 Ind. Admin. Code 6-2.1-17) with 6-foot enclosure (675 Ind. Admin. Code 20-2-26(f)), lifeguard at 2,000 sq ft, signs and equipment; no lease-notice statute (G5036).
- `portable-cooling-device`: `edu-no-portable-cooling-rule-in`; Absent; Absent (G5035): no tenant cooling-device right or restriction rule; air conditioning maintained if provided (Ind. Code § 32-31-8-5(4)(D)).
- `portable-solar`: `edu-no-portable-solar-right-in`; Absent; Absent as a tenant right (G4029); HOA solar petition is for member homeowners (Ind. Code § 32-25.5-3.5-4); investor-owned utility interconnection review before parallel operation (170 Ind. Admin. Code 4-4.3-6).

- `promises-to-repair`: `edu-promises-to-repair-in`; Absent; Absent: no writing or completion-date rule (G1030); lease promises become part of the delivery duty (Ind. Code § 32-31-8-5(1)); consumer act misrepresentation items and reasonable-time presumption (Ind. Code § 24-5-0.5-3(b)).

- `repair-escrow-exemption-notice`: `edu-no-repair-escrow-exemption-in`; Absent; Absent (G6036): no rent escrow, so no small-landlord exemption or notice; tenant repair remedy is notice then suit (Ind. Code § 32-31-8-6).
- `repair-notice`: `edu-repair-notice-in`; Present; Present: tenant notice required but no form (Ind. Code § 32-31-8-6); liability from notice or actual knowledge; written notice only for smoke detectors (Ind. Code § 22-11-18-3.5, Ind. Code § 22-11-18-5.5) and protected written complaints (G1032).

- `security-deposit-standards`: `edu-no-security-deposit-standards-in`; Absent; Absent (G3007): no rule to post or disclose deposit standards; no cap; fair housing bars differing terms on protected grounds.
- `security-devices`: `edu-no-security-devices-rule-in`; Absent; Absent (G5029): no required security devices or rekeying; protected-tenant lock change in 48 or 24 hours (Ind. Code § 32-31-9-9, Ind. Code § 32-31-9-10).

- `sprinkler-disclosure`: `edu-no-sprinkler-disclosure-in`; Absent; Absent (G5022): no rental sprinkler disclosure or retrofit incentive; nursing-home guide disclosure only (Ind. Code § 16-28-11-6).
- `statutory-caps`: `edu-statutory-caps-in`; Present; Present: collected figure caps ($20 plus bank charge for dishonored checks; water/sewer sub-billing at cost with $4 admin fee and cost-capped set-up and NSF fees under 170 IAC art. 15; $15 submeter test; $5 registration; $250 emergency-call penalty; sewer deposit 3 months; township 1 month/180 days; 60-day lien; $10,000 small claims; SCRA $2,400 adjusted); finding on water-sewer-billing-disclosure-in.
- `statutory-early-termination`: `edu-statutory-early-termination-in`; Present; Present: protected victims (Ind. Code § 32-31-9-12) and servicemembers (FEDERAL: 50 U.S.C. § 3955, extended to Guard by Ind. Code § 10-16-7-23); no fraud, forfeiture or other statutory termination ground (G2005).

- `steam-radiator-covers`: `edu-no-steam-radiator-cover-rule-in`; Absent; Absent (G5033): no radiator cover rule; heating system duty if provided (Ind. Code § 32-31-8-5(4)(D)).
- `stove-refrigerator`: `edu-no-stove-refrigerator-rule-in`; Absent; Absent (G5032): no stove or refrigerator supply duty; appliances supplied as an inducement must be maintained (Ind. Code § 32-31-8-5(4)(F)).

- `tax-escalation`: `tax-insurance-escalation-la` (tag); Tagged; Tagged tax-insurance-escalation-la (optional): no Indiana tax pass-through statute (G3033); a tenant reimbursing taxes under a lease is a property-tax "taxpayer" for appeals (Ind. Code § 6-1.1-15-0.8).
- `tenancy-at-will`: `edu-tenancy-at-will-in`; Present; Present: tenancy at will needs an express contract, ends on one month's written notice delivered to the tenant, no notice on waste; consent occupancy otherwise month to month (G7003: term used only in Ind. Code § 32-31-1-1 and § 32-31-1-8).

- `tenant-insurance-claims`: `edu-no-tenant-insurance-claims-in`; Absent; Absent: no accidental-fire or anti-subrogation statute for tenants (G8038); statutory tenant damage duties and notice-before-suit apply (Ind. Code § 32-31-7-5, Ind. Code § 32-31-7-7); liability is case law.
- `tenant-portal`: `edu-no-tenant-portal-rule-in`; Absent; Absent (G7023): no portal law; UETA applies only by agreement, a tenant may refuse other electronic transactions (non-waivable), records must be retainable and printable, specified notice methods still apply; portal fees are rent for deposits. - `tenant-security-cameras`: `edu-no-tenant-camera-rule-in`; Absent; Absent as a rental rule (G7024): lease decides; unlawful surveillance (unattended recording camera without owner or tenant consent), interception felony for audio, HOA license-plate-reader ban from July 1, 2026.
- `tpa-exemption-notice`: `edu-no-tpa-exemption-notice-in`; Absent; Absent (G6033): nothing to be exempt from, so no exemption notice; preemption exception for subsidized housing and scope exclusions need no lease notice.

- `trespass-notice`: `edu-trespass-notice-in`; Present; Present: criminal trespass bars entry or refusal to leave by a person without a contractual interest after denial (personal, posted, order, purple marks); tenants excluded; no express guest defense, case law; cautious written notice.

- `written-notice-required`: `edu-written-notice-required-in`; Present; Present: written notices listed (at-will termination, 30-day modification, 45-day deposit list, agent disclosure, sale and manager notice, protected-tenant lock and termination notices, smoke detector notice); termination and rent notices not stated as written but served on paper; electronic writing only by agreement (Ind. Code § 26-2-8-104(b), Ind. Code § 26-2-8-107).

### §4 Decisions and questions (main pass)

**Item 8, three central shared edits, confirmed (rule 62):**
- **`smoking-policy`: confirmed.**
  - Indiana's smoking chapter bans smoking in a 'public place', 'an enclosed area of a structure in which the public is invited or permitted' (Ind. Code § 7.1-5-12-2), and in a 'place of employment' (Ind. Code § 7.1-5-12-4(a)). It defines 'smoking' by lighted tobacco only (Ind. Code § 7.1-5-12-3), and lets localities be stricter (Ind. Code § 7.1-5-12-13).
  - No Indiana statute limits or governs a lease's smoking or vaping ban: G3 (whole Indiana Code 2026 search, 69 sections). Every hit pairing a smoke word with a tenancy word is a smoke-detector section (Ind. Code ch. 22-11-18, Ind. Code § 32-31-5-7, Ind. Code § 32-31-7-5), the rental inspection list (Ind. Code § 36-1-20-4.1), the smoking chapter itself, or off point. The "smoke-free housing" term had 0 hits.
  - The 2026-10-04 edit only narrows whose smoking the tenant pays for, so it can't breach Indiana law.
- **`no-alterations`: confirmed.**
  - The carve-out for "any repair, installation, rekeying, or reasonable modification that applicable law entitles Tenant to perform" (the clause's words) covers the protected tenant's lock change, which the tenant may do 'without the landlord's permission' if the landlord misses the deadline (Ind. Code § 32-31-9-11(b)). It also covers disability modifications, since discrimination includes 'A refusal to permit, at the expense of the person with a disability, reasonable modifications of existing premises' (Ind. Code § 22-9.5-5-5(c)(1)).
  - The owner of multitenant real estate may not 'Prevent a communications service provider from installing on the premises communications service equipment that an occupant requests' (Ind. Code § 8-1-32.6-9(a)(1)), subject to reasonable conditions (Ind. Code § 8-1-32.6-9(b)). That is a duty owed to the provider and occupant, and the clause defers to applicable law; antenna installations in exclusive-use areas are governed by FEDERAL: 47 C.F.R. § 1.4000.
  - The clause's ownership sentence only defers to applicable law, and Indiana makes no tenant installation the tenant's property by statute.
  - `edu-no-ev-charging-rule-in` remains the tenant-right answer on EV charging. The batch 4 EV and portable-solar rows say the same.
- **`addendum-precedence`: confirmed.** Its last limb makes every disclosure applicable law requires control over a conflicting lease term. That covers Indiana's flood disclosure (`flood-disclosure-in`), the military installation disclosure (`military-installation-disclosure-in`), the water and sewer billing disclosure (`water-sewer-billing-disclosure-in`, a separate signed page), the smoke-detector acknowledgment (`smoke-detector-acknowledgment-in`) and the federal lead disclosure (FEDERAL: 42 U.S.C. § 4852d; FEDERAL: 40 C.F.R. § 745.113).

**Item 8, `hoa-compliance`: no conflict.**
- Indiana's 2026 association fines section lets an association fine 'a member' only after the board adopts a schedule of fines and gives the member notice (Ind. Code § 32-25.5-3-12(a), Ind. Code § 32-25.5-3-12(f), P.L. 155-2026, in force July 1, 2026). It binds the association and its member (the owner), not the lease.
- No section of the condominium or homeowners association articles pairs fines with a tenant, lessee or occupant word (scripted screen of every section of Ind. Code art. 32-25 and Ind. Code art. 32-25.5).
- Deposit: a fine the lease makes the tenant's responsibility is a payment 'under a rental agreement', so it is 'rent' for the deposit chapter (Ind. Code § 32-31-3-6). An unpaid fine can therefore come from the deposit as 'rent in arrearage under the rental agreement' (Ind. Code § 32-31-3-13(2)(A)) on the itemized list (Ind. Code § 32-31-3-14).
- The clause's "fines incurred" reaches only fines lawfully assessed. No wording change is needed. The `hoa` topic rows (batch 4) state the association-side rules.
- The batch 4 helper found no conflict either. Separately, it filed a `finding_existing` (§6): the existing IN note segment on `hoa-compliance` overstates the 2026 child-care rule, which reaches only governing documents adopted or amended after June 30, 2026, and the segment omits the fine-schedule rule and Ind. Code § 32-25-1-2. Replacement wording is proposed; the clause text stands.

**Item 9:**
- **(a):** proposed `early-termination-in` (§6).
- **(b):** proposed first sentence for `casualty-termination-in` (§6).
- **(c):** CARES. No IN eviction row contradicts `edu-cares-act-notice`, but five state Indiana timelines without the federal overlay. A one-sentence pointer is proposed for each (§6).

**Decided by Claude under rule 76:**
- **`fee-in-lieu-of-deposit` (a question from batch 3): no Indiana clause is offered.**
  - No Indiana statute authorizes or regulates a fee or insurance product taken in place of a security deposit.
  - Whether a particular program is a security deposit in another form (Ind. Code § 32-31-3-9) is case law, not read (class decision 4).
  - Offering it would be the risky reading, so the cautious course is the education row `edu-no-fee-in-lieu-of-deposit-in`. Not offering adds no new product feature, so this is a legal call, not a product decision for Taylor.
- **`purpose-limitation` (batch 7): no self-help re-entry clause.** Two statutes pull apart. For an indecent nuisance, 'Without process of law, the owner may make immediate entry upon the premises.' (Ind. Code § 32-30-7-25(b)). The tenant-protection chapter says 'Except as authorized by judicial order, a landlord may not deny or interfere with a tenant's access to or possession of the tenant's dwelling unit' (Ind. Code § 32-31-5-6(c)). Which controls for a residential tenant is case law, not read. The cautious course is a court order, so `edu-purpose-limitation-in` says so and no clause offers re-entry. This follows the standing lockout decision and adds no product feature, so it is a legal call, not a question for Taylor.
- **`automatic-renewal` (batch 7): a new Indiana clause rather than a tag.** The shared `automatic-renewal-ks` leaves its notice periods without minimums, so an Indiana fill-in could fall below the periodic-tenancy notice floor (Ind. Code § 32-31-1-4(b), notice 'equal to the interval between the periods') or the year-to-year floor of 'not less than three (3) months' (Ind. Code § 32-31-1-3). `automatic-renewal-in` writes the floors into the clause instead (rule 26).
- **`rent-reporting` (batch 7): the optional source was fetched.** FEDERAL: 15 U.S.C. § 6809 defines 'financial institution' only by reference to the Bank Holding Company Act list of financial activities, which is not held. So the cautious course stands: written notice before the first report of a late payment.

**New {{variables}}:** none. The new rows use five variables that are already in the library: `{{landlord_email}}`, `{{tenant_email}}` (`electronic-notice-agreement-in`), `{{monthly_rent}}`, `{{security_deposit}}` (`fees-and-charges-in`) and `{{renewal_notice_days}}` (`automatic-renewal-in`).

**Questions the helpers raised, and how each was settled:**
- fee-in-lieu-of-deposit (IN): no Indiana statute regulates a fee or insurance product in place of a deposit, and whether a given program is a security deposit in disguise (Ind. Code § 32-31-3-9) is case law. Recommendation: no Indiana fee-in-lieu clause; the education row `edu-no-fee-in-lieu-of-deposit-in` gives the cautious course. Offering one would be a new kind of optional clause for a state without an enabling statute (unlike the FL and WA rows), so it is Taylor's call. *Settled by the main pass under rule 76 (see the decision above): offering no clause adds no product feature and is the cautious legal course, so no question goes to Taylor.*

**Questions for Taylor:** none. Every point that only case law could settle is stated in its row with the cautious course (NEEDS_REVIEW policy), and no product decision arose.

### §5 Independent check

**Set-up.** The check follows rule 80, scaled down as the prompt allows: at most two rounds, one checker agent at a time. Round 1 runs in parts as pairs of batches are saved (rule 80 [NC-gaps 1]). Each checker was briefed from the delivered rows and the primary sources (`brief/CHECK.md`), never from the helper brief. Errata come back as exact old→new pairs and are applied by `apply_errata.py`, which refuses any `old` that doesn't occur exactly once. Recurring error shapes from each part go into the next part's brief and to the helpers still writing (BRIEF.md section 10).

**Round 1, part A (batches 1–2, 60 objects, all checked section-open): 8 ERROR, 33 FIX, 41 errata, all applied, 25 rows edited.**

Substantive errors fixed:
- **Lease charges and the deposit.** Two rows said a lease charge can't be taken from the deposit. That contradicts Ind. Code § 32-31-3-6 read with Ind. Code § 32-31-3-13(2)(A).
- **Disaster.** The disaster row missed the governor's commandeering and occupancy powers (Ind. Code § 10-14-3-12(g)(4), (g)(7), Ind. Code § 10-14-3-31). The battery had no limb free of tenancy words; rerun G9108 finds them.
- **Landlord's lien.** "No landlord's lien" missed the mobile home community operator's lien (Ind. Code § 16-41-27-29) and the motor vehicle lien (Ind. Code § 32-31-3-13.5).
- **Jury demand.** A jury-demand statement contradicted Ind. Small Claims Rule 4(C).
- **Retaliation exceptions.** The exceptions in Ind. Code § 32-31-8.5-5(b) were dropped.
- **Knowledge standard.** "Or should reasonably know" was dropped from Ind. Code § 24-5-0.5-3(b)(8).
- **Applicability.** Chapter 32-31-8's applicability limits were applied to other chapters.

Reproducibility:
- Every printed battery count matched the log.
- The checker re-ran every term of all 43 live G1xxx and G2xxx batteries with 0 differences.
- `citebat.py` printed raw unit ids. It was fixed, and 68 pasted ids in batches 1–4 were converted by `fixids.py`; the 15 rows changed are listed in `work/fixids-log.tsv`.

**Main-pass sweep after part A** (rule 80 [AS 11]) for the same error wordings across every saved batch:
- The duty-inflation wording ("keep ... safe, clean and habitable") turned up in three more bodies: `edu-casualty-mitigation-waivable-in` (batch 1, fixed now), `edu-no-rent-receipt-anti-waiver-in` and `edu-condemned-premises-rent-in`. The last two are fixed after part B, so the errata don't collide.
- Every "not held / not read" statement was screened; none names a held source wrongly.

**Round 1, part B (batches 3-4: 67 objects, 55 fully section-open, 12 with secondary sections quote-matched): 11 ERROR, 25 FIX. All 36 errata applied, 23 objects edited.**

Substantive errors fixed:
- **Fee as rent**, three rows: a notice fee, a cost schedule, and the collection-fee clause's notes.
- **Mobile home community law missed.** The state licence (Ind. Code § 16-41-27-18, with a misdemeanor at Ind. Code § 16-41-27-34), the ejection grounds (Ind. Code § 16-41-27-30) and the 180-day closure notice (Ind. Code § 16-41-27-35). The batteries lacked that vocabulary; reruns G9204 and G9205 hit them.
- **Subsidized housing told the local fee bar applies**, against Ind. Code § 32-31-1-20(a).
- **Grace period and carve-outs omitted.** The January 1, 2028 grace period (Ind. Code § 36-1-20-3.6(c)) and the pre-2018 short-term rental exemption (Ind. Code § 36-1-24-1(a)) were left out.
- **Program exemption omitted.** The exemption for local programs created before July 1, 1984 was left out.
- **Knowledge words dropped** from Ind. Code § 24-5-0.5-3(b), including in the `rent-concession-wi` tag.
- **"May" written as "must"** for voucher abatement (FEDERAL: 24 C.F.R. § 982.404) and unclaimed-property penalties (Ind. Code § 32-34-1.5-71(b)).

Reproducibility: every pasted count matched the log, and all 385 terms of the 50 live batteries re-ran with identical hit lists.

**Main-pass actions after part B:**
- `errata-main-2` applied: one more duty-inflation fix. Two others had already been fixed by part B.
- **Preemption sweep** (part B shape 2): 21 bodies that state the Ind. Code § 32-31-1-20 bar now carry the subsidized-housing exception. One wrongly-added sentence was reverted, because the retaliation bar in Ind. Code § 32-31-8.5-6 has no such exception.
- **Two `finding_existing` objects** for existing rows outside the errata scope: `edu-no-just-cause-in` (mobile home ejection grounds and closure notice) and `edu-rental-registration-inspection-in` (mobile home licensing).

**Round 1, part C (batch 5, utilities and building safety: 41 objects, all checked section-open for their controlling sections, 14 with some secondary sections quote-matched only): 9 ERROR, 30 FIX. All 39 errata applied, 20 objects edited.**

Substantive errors fixed:
- **Mobile home community lighting missed.** "No statute sets lighting levels" was wrong: 'Every part of a mobile home community must be lighted at night.' (Ind. Code § 16-41-27-17), with levels in 410 Ind. Admin. Code 6-6-5. G5028 had no mobile home term; rerun G9305 hits both.
- **City utility lien exclusions dropped.** The body omitted the exclusions in Ind. Code § 8-1.5-3-8(j) and the records condition. The lead service line rule was applied to every owner, though Ind. Code § 8-1-31.6-6(d) excludes multi-family property with more than four units.
- **Fire code overstated.** 675 Ind. Admin. Code 22-2.5-12 deletes some sections of the model code's existing-buildings chapter but amends Section 1104.23, so the chapter survives in part.
- **Smoke detector exemption qualifier dropped** in two rows: the exemption reaches only a sprinklered building 'conforming to Uniform Building Code standards at the time of construction' (Ind. Code § 22-11-18-2(b)).
- **Boiler permits** run two years for heating boilers (675 Ind. Admin. Code 30-3-9(b)(2)), with the small-building exemption in 675 Ind. Admin. Code 30-1-2(f)(1); not "annual".
- **Asbestos inspection is a duty**, not a precaution, before demolition or renovation of any building other than a residential building of four or fewer units (326 Ind. Admin. Code 14-10-1(a)(1)), with notice, fees and licensing.

Main FIX items: the Commission's disaster power over utility service practices (Ind. Code § 8-1-2-113, amended by P.L. 36-2026, effective upon passage); the owner's designated address for sewer and storm water delinquency notices (Ind. Code § 36-9-23-32(c), Ind. Code § 8-1.5-5-29(d)); the 30-day mobile home community water disconnection notice (Ind. Code § 16-41-27-10(b)); "only through a submeter" corrected to the statute's 'may install' (Ind. Code § 8-1.5-3-8 is not a general bar); "may" corrected to 'shall' twice; stale "not held" notes rewritten where the source is now held.

Reproducibility:
- Every pasted battery citation in batch 5 matches `citebat.py` (43 battery names).
- The checker re-ran all 255 terms of the 30 live G50xx batteries. 238 hit lists are identical. **17 differ only by added hits** from 440 units fetched after those batteries ran (675 IAC 16 and 30, 326 IAC 14 and 18, 327 IAC 8, and CFR sections). No hit was lost. This is scope growth, not a mismatch: every battery record in `work/batteries.jsonl` carries the `unit_count` and `unit_hash` of the unit set it ran on (12 distinct sets in this pass, from 83,376 to 87,520 units), and each printed count reproduces against that set. The new hits are the sources the part C errata now cite. The final re-run (below) re-runs every live battery on the final unit set and reviews each added hit against the row's claim.

Recurring shapes passed to part D and the helpers still writing: mobile home community law (ch. 16-41-27, 410 IAC 6-6) on every housing-condition absence; stale "not held" notes after a fetch; exceptions kept in the notes but dropped from the body; "may" and "shall" in both directions; "only" inferred from a permission.

**Round 1, part D (batch 6, disclosures, notices and lease content: 35 rows, all checked section-open for their controlling sections, plus 19 finding objects): 3 ERROR, 50 FIX. All 53 errata applied, 23 rows edited.**

Substantive errors fixed:
- **Notice of sale is a release, not a duty, and it has a tail.** The landlord 'is relieved of liability ... as to events occurring after written notice to the tenant of the conveyance', but 'for one (1) year after giving notice of the conveyance, the landlord remains liable to the tenant for the security deposit' unless the buyer assumes it and takes the deposit (Ind. Code § 32-31-3-19(a)). The good-faith-sale and 'Unless otherwise agreed' conditions were also restored.
- **Retaliation exception dropped.** A landlord may sue for possession when 'The tenant is in default with respect to rent due and has failed to cure the default' (Ind. Code § 32-31-8.5-5(c)(2)); a body had made a nonpayment termination "subject to the bar on retaliation".
- **Mobile home community sanitation missed.** "No state sanitary code for rentals" missed the health and sanitation standards for mobile home communities (Ind. Code § 16-41-27-1(1), Ind. Code § 16-41-27-8(a)(1), 410 Ind. Admin. Code 6-6). Neither has a variance procedure, so the row's no-notice answer stands.

Main FIX items: the E-SIGN point restated as unsettled in the bodies as well as the notes (a uniform UETA enactment may displace E-SIGN's consent rules under FEDERAL: 15 U.S.C. § 7002(a)(1)); two other cautious courses that the bodies stated as law; dropped qualifiers (the sex offender rule's 'knowingly or intentionally', the repaired-damage notice in Ind. Code § 32-31-7-7(c)); "may record" corrected to 'shall execute and record'; a broker-definition quote that stopped at "; and"; a stale "not reviewed" note on animal statutes that are held; the federal lead disclosure's signature and same-language rules; mobile home posted rules and the closure notice's deceptive-act consequence. One absence claim had no battery; the checker's G9401 confirms it (8 hits, off point) and is now cited.

All 19 finding objects were judged correct and source-quoted; two had gaps, fixed by errata.

Reproducibility: every pasted battery citation matches `citebat.py` (G4007, G6001 to G6044; all 15 failed-run placeholders filled). Of 45 batteries re-run, 43 are identical; G6026 and G6027 gain only hits from three asbestos definition units fetched after they ran (scope growth; G6027 gives 87 sections on its logged unit set and 90 today). None of the added hits is a tenant notice, and the row says so.

**Main-pass actions after part D:**
- **Batch 8 helper corrections.** 8 corrections to its own saved rows were verified against the cited sections and applied (`work/errata-b8.jsonl`), with two topic-list additions. The main pass added two errata on `edu-tenant-confidential-information-in` (`errata-main-5`, `-5b`): the helper's new sentence on the landlord's licensee dropped 'that could not be discovered by a reasonable and timely inspection' (Ind. Code § 25-34.1-10-10(d)), and the notes now quote the sources the body relies on.
- **Battery scope labels in plain words.** `citebat.py` printed a regex scope ("extra sources matching ^IAC-...") into battery citations, which put raw unit ids into 33 notes in batches 5 and 8. It now prints "the held Indiana Administrative Code rules", "the held 675 Ind. Admin. Code rules" or "the held 410 Ind. Admin. Code rules on mobile home communities". The pasted labels were converted mechanically; every change is in `work/scope-label-log.tsv` (30 objects, listed in `check/scope-label-edited.txt`). Counts and section lists are unchanged.

**Round 1, part E (batch 7, tenancy types and lease terms, renewal, entry, keys, cameras, guests, parking, quiet possession, cannabis, firearms, purpose limitation and trespass: 30 rows and 3 tags, all checked section-open for their controlling sections): 7 ERROR, 52 FIX. All 59 errata applied, 26 objects edited (25 rows, 1 tag).**

Substantive errors fixed:
- **Long-lease formality missed.** "No seal, notarization or special signing formality" was wrong. A lease 'for a term exceeding three (3) years', paper or electronic, 'must: (1) be in writing; (2) be executed or signed by the: (A) lessor or landlord' and '(3) have an acknowledgment ... or a proof' (Ind. Code § 32-21-1-13). The battery searched "longer than" and "more than"; the statute says "exceeding". Rerun G9504 hits it. The main pass then fixed the same omission in batch 6 (`edu-lease-content-requirements-in`, `errata-main-6`) and filed a §6 finding with a full proposed row for the existing `edu-lease-recording-in`.
- **Renewal clause floor gap.** `automatic-renewal-in` gave renewal terms longer than one month and up to three months only the fill-in notice; read as periodic tenancies, they need notice equal to the period (Ind. Code § 32-31-1-4). The floor is now one full renewal period for any term of three months or less.
- **A helper correction broke its neighbour.** The inserted 180-day mobile home closure sentence came just before "the clause sets notice floors that meet each of these rules", which became false (the clause's longest floor is three months).
- **Retaliation inflated and its exceptions dropped** in two rows: the retaliatory acts are a closed list (Ind. Code § 32-31-8.5-4), with the exceptions in Ind. Code § 32-31-8.5-5(b)(1) and (c).
- **Trespass scope stretched.** "Reaches only a person without a contractual interest" is true of some subdivisions only; a tenant can trespass by entering a locked area without permission (Ind. Code § 35-43-2-2(b)(12)).
- **Smokable hemp is a crime of its own** (Ind. Code § 35-48-4-10.1(a), a Class A misdemeanor), not a lawful hemp product; fixed in the cannabis row and, as a FIX, in the `cannabis-restrictions-ak` tag's segment.

Substantive errors fixed:
- **Sex offender residency rule reversed.** The body said the restriction "does not apply" to an offender against children with two or more unrelated convictions. 'This subsection does not apply to an offender against children who has two (2) or more unrelated convictions' (Ind. Code § 35-42-4-11(d)) refers to the petition subsection: such an offender loses the right to petition, and the residency offense in Ind. Code § 35-42-4-11(c) reaches every offender against children. The batch 6 disclosure row states the residency offense without that exception and is consistent.
- **Two record-keeping duties missed.** A furnished apartment house, boarding house or rooming house 'shall keep a register, entry book, or card filing system' of 'every individual occupying any part of the premises' (Ind. Code § 16-41-29-1; a reckless violation is a Class A misdemeanor, Ind. Code § 16-41-29-5), and 'Each mobile home community operator shall maintain a register' (Ind. Code § 16-41-27-31). G8040 searched only with landlord or tenant words; the checker's G9604, with none, hits both.

Item 11 (construction liens) confirmed: the 'immediate use or benefit' limit (Ind. Code § 32-28-3-2), the no-lien contract categories and 5-day recording (Ind. Code § 32-28-3-1(e)-(f)) and the row's "only" are right, and Indiana has no notice of nonresponsibility (G8007 re-runs identically; the checker's wider G9605 finds nothing). FIXes add the credit extension to the one-year deadline (Ind. Code § 32-28-3-6), the attorney's-fee bar when the owner has paid (Ind. Code § 32-28-3-14(b)) and the owner-occupied double (Ind. Code § 32-28-3-1(h)).

### §6 Findings on existing rows

- **`edu-dv-protections-in`**: The body's last-but-one sentence, "you need not account for the deposit until 45 days after all tenancies end", states only Ind. Code § 32-31-9-13 and omits 'Notwithstanding section 13 of this chapter, a protected individual is entitled to deposits, returns, and other refunds as if the tenancy terminated by expiring under the terms of the rental agreement.' (Ind. Code § 32-31-9-12(e)). Read alone, it invites the landlord to hold the protected tenant's share, which risks 'all of the security deposit due the tenant and reasonable attorney's fees' (Ind. Code § 32-31-3-12(b)). Proposed replacement sentence for that row's body: "Other adult tenants stay bound. The protected tenant is entitled to deposit refunds as if the tenancy had expired, even though you otherwise need not account for the deposit until 45 days after all tenancies end; see the deposit-timing row for the safe course." Pointer: `edu-dv-deposit-timing-in`. No other field changes.
- **`edu-no-deposit-holding-rule-in`**: The row's body, "Indiana does not require a security deposit to be kept in a separate, trust or escrow account or at a particular kind of institution.", omits a duty that applies when a licensed real estate broker company holds the deposit: each broker company shall 'Deposit into a trust account described in subdivision (1) all funds belonging to others that come into the possession of the broker company or of any broker whose license the broker company is holding.' (Ind. Code § 25-34.1-4-5(a)) and 'shall not use any trust account for the deposit of any personal funds or other business funds' (Ind. Code § 25-34.1-4-5(b)); a broker includes one who for consideration 'leases, rents, manages' real estate (Ind. Code § 25-34.1-1-2). The row's notes also record only a whole read of the deposit chapter and "no targeted whole-code battery run". Proposed row for Claude Code to add (do not edit the existing row): id `edu-broker-held-deposits-in`; group Security Deposit; title Deposits Held by a Real Estate Broker Company; states IN; is_active TRUE; supersedes empty; rule_type CONDITIONAL; content_type LANDLORD_EDUCATION; verification_status VERIFIED; effective_from and last_checked the day added; choice_group and is_default empty; topic_key security-deposit-holding; lease_clause_basis empty; bodyText: "Indiana's deposit law itself does not say where a security deposit must be kept. But if a licensed real estate broker company manages the property and the deposit or any other tenant money comes into its hands, Indiana's real estate licensing law requires the broker company to put it in a clearly identified trust account, keep a record of the funds held for each person, never mix it with personal or business funds, and hold any interest earned for the person the money belongs to. An owner who manages the property and holds the deposit personally is not covered by that rule."; notes: "IN: Ind. Code § 25-34.1-4-5(a), Ind. Code § 25-34.1-4-5(b), Ind. Code § 25-34.1-1-2 (broker, broker company). The Real Estate Commission's current rules were not read. Read section-open from the Indiana corpus." Found while answering holding-deposit (batch 3).
- **`water-sewer-billing-disclosure-in`**: The clause's fee brackets, "a set-up fee of [amount]" and "a fee of [amount] for a payment of these charges returned for insufficient funds", carry no limit, and its notes say only that electric submetering's agency rule was not read; the water and sewer sub-billing rules in 170 Ind. Admin. Code art. 15 (held, read whole) were not applied. Those rules cap both figures: the set-up fee is 'not to exceed the landlord's or association's actual administrative cost.' (170 Ind. Admin. Code 15-1-3) and 'An insufficient funds fee shall not exceed the actual fee assessed to a landlord or an association by a financial institution' (170 Ind. Admin. Code 15-1-4(b)). They also impose landlord duties the library does not state: 'Provide a period of at least seventeen (17) days from the date the landlord or association sends a sub-bill for the tenant, member, or co-owner to remit payment before initiating a collection action for sub-billing charges.' (170 Ind. Admin. Code 15-2-0.5(a)); keep bills and sub-bills 'for a period of not less than two (2) years' (170 Ind. Admin. Code 15-2-1(a)); list on each sub-bill the items in 170 Ind. Admin. Code 15-2-3(b), including the commission contact '(800) 851-4268.' (170 Ind. Admin. Code 15-2-3(b)) and the due date 'with a statement that the sub-bill due date is independent from the due date of the lease payment or other charges.' (170 Ind. Admin. Code 15-2-3(b)); and adjust errors in the tenant's favor within 90 days (170 Ind. Admin. Code 15-2-5(a)). A landlord that does not produce records within 15 days of the consumer affairs division's request, or ignores a determination for 90 days, 'is regulated as a public utility' (170 Ind. Admin. Code 15-3-3(b)). The rules do not apply to flat-fee distribution or a single-unit building metered by the utility (170 Ind. Admin. Code 15-1-0.5(c)). Proposed row for Claude Code to add (do not edit the existing clause): id `edu-water-sewer-sub-billing-in`; group Rent & Payment; title Rules for Billing Tenants Separately for Water and Sewer; states IN; is_active TRUE; supersedes empty; rule_type REQUIRED/CONDITIONAL; content_type LANDLORD_EDUCATION; verification_status VERIFIED; effective_from and last_checked the day added; choice_group and is_default empty; topic_key utility-submetering-disclosure; lease_clause_basis empty; bodyText: "If you bill tenants separately for water or sewer service you buy from a utility, the Indiana Utility Regulatory Commission's rules apply on top of the statute. Your usage charges must be worked out by one of the commission's methods (actual sub-meter readings, or a reasonable allocation by unit, square footage, type of unit, number of occupants or estimated use, after deducting your own and common-area use) and may never total more than you paid. A set-up fee may not exceed your actual administrative cost, a returned-payment fee may not exceed what your bank actually charged you, and the administrative fee may not exceed $4 a month. Bill on a consistent schedule; give the tenant at least 17 days after sending a bill before starting collection; show on each bill the items the rules list, including the commission's phone number and website and a statement that the bill's due date is separate from the rent due date; keep utility bills and sub-bills for at least two years and give a tenant copies within three business days of asking, for at most a nominal copying fee; and correct billing errors in the tenant's favor within 90 days of learning of them, for up to one year back. If you do not answer the commission's consumer affairs division within 15 days, or ignore its determination for 90 days, you are regulated as a public utility and may not charge tenants until you fix the problem. These rules do not apply to a flat fee that includes water without itemizing it, or to a single-unit building the utility meters directly."; notes: "IN: 170 Ind. Admin. Code 15-1-0.5, 15-1-3, 15-1-4, 15-2-0.5, 15-2-1, 15-2-2, 15-2-3, 15-2-5, 15-3-3 (art. 15 read whole from the held Administrative Code sources); Ind. Code § 8-1-2-1.2(k)-(m). Related: `water-sewer-billing-disclosure-in`, `edu-statutory-caps-in`." The main pass may also wish to have the clause's two fee brackets carry the caps, as `returned-payments-in` does for the check fee. Found while answering statutory-caps (batch 3).
- **`hoa-compliance`**: IN segment check (batch 4, hoa topic): no conflict with the clause text, but the IN segment overstates the child care rule and omits the 2026 fine and condominium rules. (1) Child care: the segment says an association "may not restrict child care in a single-family residence the provider rents", citing Ind. Code § 32-25.5-3.9-5; the chapter 'applies only to a homeowners association's adoption or amendment of governing documents after June 30, 2026.' (Ind. Code § 32-25.5-3.9-1(a)), excludes qualifying age-restricted communities (Ind. Code § 32-25.5-3.9-1(b)), and protects only a person providing child care in 'a single family residence that the person resides within and owns, rents, or leases.' (Ind. Code § 32-25.5-3.9-5). (2) Fines: from July 1, 2026, for every homeowners association (Ind. Code § 32-25.5-1-1(b)), 'A homeowners association may assess a fine for a member's violation of a covenant described in IC 32-25.5-2-3(2) if the board first adopts a schedule of fines that sets forth:' (Ind. Code § 32-25.5-3-12(a)) and gives the member notice (Ind. Code § 32-25.5-3-12(f)); the clause passes on only fines incurred, so read it as reaching only a fine lawfully assessed. (3) Condominium tenants are bound directly: 'Tenants of condominium unit owners.' (Ind. Code § 32-25-1-2(a)). (4) Deposit: a passed-on fine paid to the landlord under the lease is rent ('all payments made to a landlord under a rental agreement except a security deposit' (Ind. Code § 32-31-3-6)), and the deposit may pay 'all rent in arrearage under the rental agreement' (Ind. Code § 32-31-3-13(2)), so no deposit conflict. Proposed replacement IN segment: "IN: Applies as written. Condominium tenants are bound by the condominium article and the association's declaration and bylaws (Ind. Code § 32-25-1-2(a)); in a homeowners association the tenant's duty comes from this clause. From July 1, 2026 a homeowners association fines the member only under an adopted schedule of fines and after notice (Ind. Code § 32-25.5-3-12, P.L. 155-2026; all associations, Ind. Code § 32-25.5-1-1(b)); read the clause as reaching only a fine lawfully assessed for a violation by Tenant or Tenant's household or guests. A reimbursed fine is rent under Ind. Code § 32-31-3-6 and may be taken from the deposit as rent in arrearage (Ind. Code § 32-31-3-13(2)). For governing documents adopted or amended after June 30, 2026, an association may not restrict child care by a person who lives in and rents a detached single-family home (Ind. Code § 32-25.5-3.9-1, Ind. Code § 32-25.5-3.9-5, P.L. 53-2026); that binds the association, not the landlord. See `edu-hoa-in`. Read section-open 2026-11-12 from the Indiana corpus (2026 Indiana Code, iga.in.gov whole-title files; corpus README)." Fields for Claude Code: row `hoa-compliance`, notes only; replace the existing IN segment (beginning "IN: Applies as written. A homeowners association may not restrict child care") with the text above; no bodyText, rule_type or states change.
- **`edu-alarm-duties-in`**: Precision, not a duty error. The row's body says "a totally sprinkled building that met building code standards when built is exempt from the smoke detector chapter". The statute names a specific model code: 'A totally sprinkled building (conforming to Uniform Building Code standards at the time of construction) is exempt from the requirements of this chapter.' (Ind. Code § 22-11-18-2(b)). Proposed replacement text for that clause: "a totally sprinkled building that met Uniform Building Code standards (an older model code) when built is exempt from the smoke detector chapter, though the move-in acknowledgment is still required". Its notes also say the chapter 22-11-16 scope and the commission's rules were "not read"; the scope is now read and stated in `edu-fire-code-standard-in` (apartments 'in buildings containing three (3) or more apartments' (Ind. Code § 22-11-16-2(b)(1)), excluding an apartment 'from which apartment there is immediate ground level access to the outside.' (Ind. Code § 22-11-16-2(d))). No new row needed; see `edu-frozen-standard-incorporation-in`.
- **`edu-notice-service-in`**: Update, not an error: the row says Indiana's electronic transactions law's "rules on when electronic delivery is effective were not reviewed here" (row words). They have now been read whole (Ind. Code § 26-2-8-104, Ind. Code § 26-2-8-107, Ind. Code § 26-2-8-114) for `edu-actual-notice-method-in`: the act 'only applies to transactions between parties each of which has agreed to conduct transactions electronically.' (Ind. Code § 26-2-8-104(b)), and a record another law requires to be sent by a specified method 'must be sent, communicated, or transmitted by the method specified in the other law.' (Ind. Code § 26-2-8-107(b)(2)). Proposed: replace the row's last sentence with "Indiana's electronic transactions law lists no exclusion for landlord-tenant notices, but it applies only if both parties agreed to deal electronically and it does not replace a delivery method the law specifies; see the notice method row." and add `edu-actual-notice-method-in` to its notes. All other fields unchanged.
- **`edu-lead-safe-work-in`**: The row's notes say "The definition of 'target housing' that Ind. Code § 16-41-39.8-13 uses was not read" (row words), and its body applies the work rules to "target housing built before 1960" without the definition's exclusions. Read now: "Target housing", for purposes of lead-based paint activities and Ind. Code ch. 16-41-39.8, means 'housing constructed before January 1, 1978.' (Ind. Code § 16-18-2-346.3(a)), and the term does not include 'Housing for the elderly or individuals with disabilities that is not occupied by or expected to be occupied by a child of not more than six (6) years of age.' (Ind. Code § 16-18-2-346.3(b)(1)) or 'A building without a bedroom.' (Ind. Code § 16-18-2-346.3(b)(2)). The section also applies, for an owner's own dwelling, when it is occupied by a child who 'resides in the building and has been identified as having an elevated blood lead level.' (Ind. Code § 16-41-39.8-13(b)(2)(B)). Proposed bodyText addition after the first sentence: "These rules do not apply to housing for the elderly or for people with disabilities where no child aged six or younger lives or is expected to live, or to a building without a bedroom." Proposed notes: replace the not-read sentence with the definition quotation above and its citation, and change the basis line to the 2026-11-12 corpus read. All other fields unchanged; topic_key lead-based-paint.
- **`water-sewer-billing-disclosure-in`**: Two updates now that 170 Ind. Admin. Code art. 4 and art. 15 are held (brief section 10 item 5). (1) The row's notes say electric submetering is governed by "170 Ind. Admin. Code 4-5, an agency rule not read" (row words). It is held and requires a lease statement: 'All rental agreements between the owner and the tenants of dwelling units shall clearly state that: the dwelling unit is submetered, electric bills will be based upon submeter readings, electrical consumption for all common areas and common facilities will be the responsibility of the owner and not the tenant and will describe the procedure to be followed in the event of disputes.' (170 Ind. Admin. Code 4-5-12(i)). The batch-5 clause `electric-submetering-disclosure-in` carries it. Proposed notes change for this row: replace "an agency rule not read" with "see electric-submetering-disclosure-in". (2) Water sub-billing rules: sub-bills must carry 'The statement required by IC 8-1-2-1.2(m)(2)(C), including the following contact information for the commission:' (170 Ind. Admin. Code 15-2-3(b)(16)), (800) 851-4268 and www.in.gov/iurc, and the landlord must 'Provide a period of at least seventeen (17) days from the date the landlord or association sends a sub-bill for the tenant, member, or co-owner to remit payment before initiating a collection action for sub-billing charges.' (170 Ind. Admin. Code 15-2-0.5(a)(4)). Proposed bodyText change: the bracket "[insert phone number for the Commission]" may be prefilled "(800) 851-4268 (www.in.gov/iurc)", and add after the fee list: "Each sub-bill will show the information the Commission's rules require, and Tenant will have at least 17 days after Landlord sends a sub-bill to pay it before Landlord begins any collection action." All other fields unchanged.

    - `bodyText`: Tenant may terminate this Lease before the end of the Term by providing Landlord at least 30 days' written notice. Tenant will pay Landlord, by the termination date, an early termination fee equal to one month's Rent ({{monthly_rent}}), and remains responsible for Rent and other obligations up to the termination date. If Tenant gives that notice, pays the fee and delivers possession of the property by the termination date, Tenant owes no Rent for any period after the termination date. If Tenant leaves before the end of the Term without using this option, Tenant's liability is governed by Indiana law. This early-termination option and fee apply only if this Lease has a fixed Term; a month-to-month or other periodic tenancy ends on the notice that applicable law and this Lease provide, without an early termination fee. Landlord may terminate this Lease early in accordance with this Lease's Tenant Default and notice provisions, or if Tenant abandons the property, meaning that Tenant has failed to pay or offer to pay Rent that is due and the circumstances are such that a reasonable person would conclude that Tenant has surrendered possession; except for an abandoned property, Landlord will regain possession only through a court proceeding. Nothing in this Section limits any right either party has under applicable law, including a Tenant's right to terminate without an early termination charge because of military service under the Servicemembers Civil Relief Act or Indiana law, the right of a Tenant who is a protected individual under Indiana law to end this Lease on 30 days' written notice without this fee or any other rent or fees due only because of the early termination, or a Tenant's rights if the property becomes unfit to live in through no fault of Tenant.
    - `notes`: IN: Proposed override of early-termination-ks (IN gaps pass 2026-11-12, Indiana item 9(a); IN gaps log §6). Flat one-month fee instead of the greater of one month's Rent or 30% of the remaining Rent: no Indiana statute governs a residential early-termination fee, and whether an agreed fee is an unenforceable penalty is Indiana case law, not read (IN gaps log §4, class decision 4); a flat fee measured by one rental period is the cautious course the library uses in Georgia, Pennsylvania, Alabama, Utah and Missouri. Deposit: a security deposit may be used to pay 'rent due for premature termination of the rental agreement by the tenant' (Ind. Code § 32-31-3-13(2)(B)), and in that chapter 'rent' includes 'all payments made to a landlord under a rental agreement except a security deposit, however denominated' (Ind. Code § 32-31-3-6). Abandonment: the shared clause's ground "if Tenant vacates or abandons the property without notifying Landlord" is replaced because 'An oral or written rental agreement may not define abandonment differently than is provided by this subsection.' (Ind. Code § 32-31-5-6(b)); a dwelling unit is abandoned only if 'the tenants have failed to' pay or offer to pay rent due and 'the circumstances are such that a reasonable person would conclude that the tenants have surrendered possession of the dwelling unit' (Ind. Code § 32-31-5-6(b)). Possession: 'Except as authorized by judicial order, a landlord may not deny or interfere with a tenant's access to or possession of the tenant's dwelling unit' (Ind. Code § 32-31-5-6(c)), and that section 'does not apply if the dwelling unit has been abandoned' (Ind. Code § 32-31-5-6(a)). Protected individuals: a protected individual who ends the lease under Ind. Code § 32-31-9-12 'is not liable for any other rent or fees that would be due only because of the early termination' (Ind. Code § 32-31-9-12(d)), on written notice 'at least thirty (30) days before the termination date stated in the notice' (Ind. Code § 32-31-9-12(b)). Military: FEDERAL: 50 U.S.C. § 3955 ('The lessor may not impose an early termination charge'); Indiana's chapter supplements the federal act (Ind. Code § 10-16-20-1). Casualty: no Indiana statute on casualty damage to a residential lease, abatement or termination, G6 (whole Indiana Code 2026 search, 80 sections): every hit is off point (insurance, public finance, the commercial code's goods-lease rule in Ind. Code § 26-1-2.1-221, the occupancy standard's fire-code reference in Ind. Code § 32-31-8-7). Read section-open 2026-11-12 from the Indiana corpus (2026 Indiana Code, iga.in.gov whole-title files; corpus README); FEDERAL: 50 U.S.C. § 3955 read 2026-11-12 from uscode.house.gov.

(1) edu-nonpayment-notice-in says the library's lease does not require notice for unpaid advance rent and that a 10-day notice may be given; (2) edu-notice-to-quit-in says a fixed-term lease ends without notice and a month-to-month tenancy on a month's notice; (3) periodic-tenancy-notice-in lets either party end on notice "at least one month before the end of a monthly rental period" (its words), which can be under 30 days; (4) edu-emergency-possessory-orders-in says the tenant must return possession within 7 days of the hearing; (5) edu-drug-nuisance-in describes a 72-hour vacate order in an owner's nuisance case. Code § 16-41-27-30): nonpayment of charges or fees, violation of law or disorderly conduct, violation of a state department rule, or violation of a posted community rule (Ind. Code § 16-41-27-30). Whether that list is exclusive is case law, not read. Closing a community needs written notice to each home owner 'not later than one hundred eighty (180) days' before closure (Ind. Code § 16-41-27-35(a)).     - `bodyText`: Indiana's statute of frauds does not apply to a lease for a term of three years or less, so an oral lease of that length can be enforced. A lease for longer than three years, on paper or as an electronic record, must be in writing, signed by the landlord, and acknowledged before a notary (or proved). It must also be recorded in the county recorder's office within 45 days after signing, or it is void against a later good-faith buyer, tenant or mortgagee.
    - `notes`: IN: 'This section does not apply to a lease for a term of not more than three (3) years.' (Ind. Code § 32-21-1-1(a)). Formality: a 'conveyance' includes any electronic record or paper that is 'a lease or memorandum of lease for a term exceeding three (3) years' (Ind. Code § 32-21-1-13(a)), and 'A conveyance must: (1) be in writing; (2) be executed or signed by the: (A) lessor or landlord' and '(3) have an acknowledgment (as defined in IC 33-42-0.5-2) or a proof (as defined in and permitted under IC 32-21-2).' (Ind. Code § 32-21-1-13(b)). Whether a long lease that lacks the acknowledgment is still enforceable between the parties is case law, not read; the cautious course is to have it acknowledged. Recording: Ind. Code § 32-31-2-1, Ind. Code § 32-31-2-2, Ind. Code § 32-21-3-3. Rule 15: written section-open. Read section-open 2026-09-30 in the built-in browser from iga.in.gov; Ind. Code § 32-21-1-13 read section-open 2026-11-12 from the Indiana corpus (2026 Indiana Code, iga.in.gov whole-title files; corpus README), found by battery G9504 (IN gaps log §5, part E).

Result: Fetched in this pass: FEDERAL: 15 U.S.C. § 6809, from uscode.house.gov. It defines 'financial institution' only by reference to the Bank Holding Company Act list of financial activities, which is not held, so the row's cautious course stands (errata-main-4).

### Proposed SOP changes

1. **Rule 24: moving a whole code through the device bridge.** Where Claude Code's shell can't reach the official site but the built-in browser can, the Desktop pass can still hold the whole code locally. The route that worked here:
   - load every whole-title file in-page;
   - serialize the parsed sections;
   - gzip and base64 them in the page (CompressionStream);
   - return about 240,000 characters per javascript call, each with 2,100 spaces of leading padding. The bridge cuts results at about 260 KB, and a result that large lands as a file rather than in the chat.
   
   The extractor here reassembles the chunks and checks two browser-computed SHA-256 hashes, one of the base64 and one of the decompressed export. Indiana's 74 MB Code moved in 89 chunks, and its 2026 Acts volume (4,892 PDF pages, through pdf.js) in 14. Both hashes reproduced on three independent page loads. A helper agent can run the chunk loop, so the main context stays small. [IN-gaps 1]
2. **Rule 19 [WV]: name the tab-reuse trap in the built-in browser.** In the built-in browser, `preview_start` can open its URL in the tab that is in front, including the crawl tab, and erase its in-memory state. It happened here mid-transfer. Open every other page with `tabs_create`, then `navigate` with that tab's id, and keep the loader source in a workspace file so the corpus can be rebuilt and its hashes compared. [IN-gaps 2]
3. **Rule 19: count checks must parse nested parentheses.** The canonical battery citation names its scopes in parentheses inside its own parentheses, for example "G1005 (search of 2026 session laws (P.L. 1-2026 to P.L. 163-2026, Acts 2026 volume); Ind. Code 2026 (whole), 71 sections)". A count checker that stops at the first ")" never compares the count. Indiana's checker let a typed 75 for 71 and a 23 for 17 through until it was fixed. Test the count check on a citation in the exact form `citebat` prints. [IN-gaps 3]
4. **Rule 27: helpers never edit a saved answers file.** A helper whose later saves were blocked by a stricter validator edited its saved batch file directly to unblock them. It kept a pre-edit copy and reported the edit, but the change still bypassed the errata trail. Give helpers a tool that files an erratum (exact old and new text) against their own saved objects, applied by the main pass's errata script with a once-only assertion. Tighten a validator only between batches, and tell running helpers what changed. [IN-gaps 4]
5. **Rule 80: an erratum script must refuse an `old` that falls inside text an earlier erratum wrote.** Here a main-pass sweep's `old` text matched inside a phrase a checker's erratum had already corrected. It applied "once" by the letter of the check and produced "make all reasonable efforts to make all reasonable efforts". Round 2 caught it. The script keeps every applied `new` and refuses a later `old` that overlaps one, unless that erratum names the earlier one it replaces. [IN-gaps 5]
6. **Rule 80 [AS 11]: a sweep sentence attaches to one bar, not to a sentence.** The subsidized-housing exception sentence was added after sentences that state two local-rule bars. One came from Ind. Code § 32-31-1-20, which has the exception; the other from Ind. Code § 36-1-3-13, which doesn't. A sweep that adds an exception names the bar it qualifies whenever the sentence before it states more than one. [IN-gaps 6]

### Proposed topic questions

- `dv-deposit-timing`: Where one section entitles a protected tenant to deposit refunds "Notwithstanding" the co-tenant section, and the co-tenant section lets the landlord hold the deposit until all tenancies end, does either section say how one deposit is divided among co-tenants? (Indiana: Ind. Code § 32-31-9-12(e) and Ind. Code § 32-31-9-13.)
- `early-termination`: Does the state bar a lease from defining abandonment differently from its statutory definition, so that a shared clause letting the landlord end the lease when the tenant "vacates ... without notifying Landlord" can't be tagged? (Indiana: Ind. Code § 32-31-5-6(b), 'An oral or written rental agreement may not define abandonment differently than is provided by this subsection.')
